ParlaMint-AT_2011-05-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00105_d7e13828	Mr President. High house! One of the principles of the Austrian waste management is the prevention of waste – which does not concern it clearly does not have to be disposed of. Secondly, a comprehensive collection of the waste is included. The third point is, as much as possible due to the substance cycle and only, which is no longer reusable, is to be reused, used thermally or deposited. The effect is that we or Austria have a top position in waste management. We are, for example, garbage world champions. The current number is that 116 kilograms per head are recorded that is separated in waste glass, waste metals, plastics, waste paper and cetera. That's why I'm saying thank you to the people. There are many young people here among the listeners, who have been educated by the parent and grandparents generation in Austria, that you do not throw out the paperwork while driving, that you collect separately and as much as possible lead back into the fabric cycle. The effect: 60 percent of the waste generated in Austria is recycled – an international peak. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the SPÖ.]] If you calculate thermal recovery, it's a total of 96 percent of total waste, that's a fact. If you look at other continents or Europe around our borders, there is no such high priority for waste management. This is a result of the policies, the players in the federal government, in the countries and especially at the municipal level, as well as the fact that people are just engaged in avoiding waste and collecting it separately. I'm holding this because you're talking about the waste economic chaos here and I'm happy to mention the plastic sack as an example – which is not so. Brunner.]] It must not be buried or deposited, that is legal condition. I'm saying this so you can see that we're in order. Korun.]] The point is that the plastic sackerls in Austria amount to 0.01 percent of the waste. Naples!]] The fact is that just there, the thin plastic sacs that enter the sea – that has already been addressed by previous speakers – are a problem for marine creatures because they swallow and destroy these parts. That is why, for a short time now, the ministers also discussed this at the European level. It is even now being discussed whether fishermen should be commissioned to fish plastic from the sea to prevent such a threat to biodiversity. – It should not be beautiful, but simply left the church in the village and instead be clarified as it actually is. The point is, of course, it makes sense to switch to alternatives that the plastic sack is not the Ultima Ratio. So I started a five-point program to have at the end of the day, so to speak, a plastic sackerl, which is divided into several levels: This includes, on the one hand, cooperation with trade in the prevention of waste – what you always demand, we implement. The second point is a pilot project with the trade with which we want to replace the plastic sackerl and use degradable materials. The third point is the formation of consciousness – that people are being informed about what they can do for it. The fourth point is the evaluation of the existing regime in EU countries; the fifth is a requirement for marking plastic carrier bags. The pilot project is under way. We managed to get 98 percent of the Austrian food trade into the boat, all the large supermarket chains are included. 98 percent of the food trade says we are testing these so-called node bags in a multi-month pilot phase. So these thin, problematic plastic sachets are replaced. The point is – and this is my responsibility, and that has been said before – that the producers of these depleted Sackerln say they are more expensive. It is part of the overall responsibility, also to the consumer, to see how he accepts this price by 10 cents per piece. We looked at it again yesterday in supermarket stores. People take that part, put it in, pay, tell others I don't care. It's an evaluation phase. In Austria, we now have about 120 branches in which this is tested, where we see how consumers react to it. I think this is a waste management policy with far-sighted vision – to bring people into the boat and then to bring them to the point of moving forward without plastic sacks. We will evaluate this pilot project – with regard to the above-mentioned consumer acceptance, but also towards disposal behaviour: Where do the disposal routes lead? We will also carry out waste analyses. You see, we have a comprehensive approach to that, too. At the European level, I have become active, and I must say to you: At the European level, I have launched the initiative to make it possible for a state to issue a plastic ban. You know it's not legal. Italy has welcomed and supported my initiative, however, with a note, not fully supported how it would have been desirable. In any event, my initiative says that we should allow the ban on plastic sacks to be legalized; that existing regulations in the Member States should be evaluated, that labelling should be introduced at European level, that checks should be made on the extent to which there are alternatives to plastic carrier bags, and that, ultimately, much should be invested in waste management programmes. The impact of the initiative at European level is quite up-to-date. The Commission establishes, this is a report from today, Stakeholder Consultations. That is to say, stakeholders, stakeholders, the economy invited by NGOs to participate in a public process when it comes to avoiding plastic packaging – participate! These topics will be discussed at European level: To set prevention targets, bans, financial measures – voluntary measures, but also taxes – as well as biodegradability and making it visible to consumers. You can communicate on the Internet and bring in the ideas you have across Europe. This is a result of my initiative at the European level, which will lead to a Green Paper from the European Commission to amend the packaging directive in the final effect, in the sense that we want it, in fact, we are all in a boat: to keep the high standard of Austrian waste management, as much as possible in the cycle of substances – and, of course, to avoid a lot of what is actually happening. [[Abg. May. Brunner: Avoid!]] Look at reality, that's it! The fact is that we are a model for Europe and for the world in the waste management sector. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-05-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00105_d7e14143	Mr President. Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The history of plastic bags – the introduction, the high stage and also the reputation – is very closely linked to the development of our society, from the consumer society to the post-material society. The way to the consumer society is of course also very much linked to the replacement of the paper bag by the plastic bag. You bought more, and it was also much more convenient to carry the things home. The paper bag was dominant until the mid-fifties, and since 1953 we have seen a very rapid rise in the plastic bag, with different types of plastics being tested during the 1950s. Finally, one has opted for the full synthetic plastic, such as polyethylene, from which almost all plastic bags are produced so far. In the 1960s, the plastic bag increasingly pushed out the paper bag, and in 1971 the market share of the plastic bag was also particularly high, it was 66 percent. You can also read from different authors that the plastic bags were expressions of the new, consumer-oriented age. Some studies also show that the use of plastic bags has greatly affected the purchasing behaviour. For example, sales in the grocery stores increased by 21 percent when people bought plastic bags. With the oil crisis 1973, the plastic bag also came into crisis. The consequence was that the slogan “Jute instead of Plastic” was increasingly used for alternative raw materials. In the end, however, the purchasing behaviour of the consumer society, the disposable society, has been geared towards convenience. The plastic bag has remained on the market. We only have a slow decline in plastic bags when the conversion of the consumer society to the post-material society has taken place. This means that the disposable product plastic has become a negative symbol for garbage and, above all, for personal co-responsibility in the prevention of waste. Technological and social development is also evident in the development of the carrier bag. It is of course that we support a very determined reduction, and ideally of course also the abolition, plastic bags and the forcing of sustainable, degradable carrier bags made of corn starch or similar products. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-05-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00105_d7e20144	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! Back to the collective report of the Committee on Petitions and Citizens' Initiatives. Don't ask me! – This is a sentence with which you, as politicians, are certainly faced more often, a sentence expressing their displeasure to some of the measures that have an impact on their immediate living environment. It will be bad if, out of this mood, a certain general political starkness arises, because the citizens feel that their experiences, their problems are not perceived by politicians at all. This creates a need for more participation, a need for more democracy. Citizens' initiatives and petitions give citizens the opportunity to participate. It is important, however, that the thresholds and the obstacles are reduced so that as many people as possible can comply with their right of petition. Of course, initiatives should also be made available to as many policy-makers as possible, so that they can also support and support them. I believe that the more citizens appear, the more they support and support initiatives, the more conscious their problems and concerns can be identified. A presentation of the process of negotiation of petitions and citizens' initiatives is already available on Parliament's homepage. A new idea is the possibility of direct citizen participation through the possibility of electronic support for the initiatives under negotiation. This e-voting or support by electronic means can revive the direct influence of the people on politics. In addition to this new offer, which is now being prepared, there are, of course, also numerous further proposals for improvement. The hearings could also be further developed as an important instrument of the Committee. Of course, there were also hearings in the last committee, on the citizens' initiative number 25 “Justice is strong – for more distribution justice!” In the hearing, the experts and MEPs also pointed out several times that many of the demands contained in them had already been implemented. Thus, the speculation period has been deleted and replaced by a Kest duty for divestment profits. The stability levy for banks is also implemented: Increase the intermediate tax to 25 percent for foundations and the measures to combat tax evasion. Incentives have also been set for environmentally friendly behaviour. Not included in the Citizens' Initiative is the demand for support for families. This issue will have to be further developed intensively. In the future, families in particular will also have to be taxed. There was also a hearing on the citizens' initiative on the valorisation of the care allowance. No question: The major challenge in this area lies in financing. The first steps have already been taken in the right direction with the establishment of the care fund. Many other highly interesting issues are addressed to the Committee. Some of these are also included in the collective report to be found on today's agenda, for example the petition 68 "Retaining the 13th. Family aid’. This was not abolished, but converted into a school start-up aid of 100 € for children aged six to 15 years. Other petitions concern transport problems, some of them also fall within the competence of the federal states, especially the state of Lower Austria. Three further petitions – “Acquisition of the ‘New Middle School’, “Children do not belong to prison” and “A rapid, human-right reform and humanitarian reform of Austrian foreign law” – could be assigned to the respective specialist committees for further negotiation. The diversity of initiatives shows that the petitioners feel that the Committee on Petitions and Citizens' Initiatives is an important parliamentary body. They expect their problems to be taken seriously and, above all, to be presented very transparently and, of course, also discussed. Together we work on a positive development in the Committee with good success. It is an important citizen platform that can, of course, require any support and, above all, any positive development. Thanks to the meetings organised to improve the Committee constantly, and also the support of all the speakers involved, we are continuing to pursue a new and, hopefully, successful path in the sense of greater transparency and closer proximity to the citizen. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-05-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00105_d7e21713	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Both agreements have a clause which provides for a revision of this Treaty after five years. This possibility was also used constructively, on the one hand with our strategic partner South Africa, on the other hand with Korea. I think it pays off to go ahead with this Korean agreement, because this is actually a very interesting development of European foreign policy and of European foreign policy. It is the first agreement of this kind, the framework agreement that we, the European Union, have concluded with an Asian partner. It includes a truly wide range of topics. They are far from human rights over Ter fight against racism, climate change and energy security. The first such framework agreement also laid the cornerstone for the Free Trade Agreement, which was recently approved in both the European Parliament and the Korean Parliament. This free trade agreement between the European Union and Korea creates the second largest agreement worldwide, even after NAFTA. Last year alone, it has included a trade volume between the partners of more than 92 billion dollars. Korea is the eighth largest trading partner for the European Union and the European Union is the second largest export market for Korea. With this free trade agreement, which was approved for the first time in accordance with the rules of the Lisbon Treaty in the European Parliament, we have actually created a commercial milestone. The European Union has recently overtaken the Americans who have also wanted to negotiate a free trade agreement with Korea for a number of years and who are not yet as far as we are. For Austria's companies, this agreement brings very concrete benefits. Export opportunities in this market with a strong growth potential are excellent. We had a 6.1 percent growth there in 2010, one of 4.5 percent in 2011. However, I do not want to end these remarks without pointing out that, of course, we need to continue working and that we should also work better on multilateral trade diplomacy. The Doha Round – which is repeatedly conjured in Sunday's speeches – brings concrete benefits, not only for the developing countries, but also for all of us. This means that the liberalisation of world trade at the multilateral level must not come to a halt. It's a shame that nothing has gone on in recent times. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-05-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00105_d7e21786	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to talk about the amendment of the agreement with South Africa. The Republic of South Africa is an extremely important cooperation and economic partner for the EU in southern Africa. It is a stable, democratic rule of law and Africa's largest economy in all the existing problems, and it has a great weight within the African Union and in the block-free movement and belongs to both the G20 and the G8+5. We were all able to observe the excellent logistical and organizational performance of the country last summer, when South Africa was the first African country to lead the World Football Championship. Close and successful cooperation with South Africa is therefore crucial for the economic, development and security interests of Austria and the EU in southern Africa. The amending agreement makes a number of important amendments to the original agreement. Firstly, the issue of disarmament and non-proliferation of weapons of mass destruction has been included as an important complement. I'm very glad. This is in full harmony with the external po Austrian security and security interests. South Africa is therefore an important partner on this issue. With the end of apartheid, there has been an existing comprehensive nuclear weapons programme with six usable nuclear bombs. These have been abandoned by South Africa. South Africa has since been involved in international disarmament and non-proliferation of weapons of mass destruction. South Africa therefore has a great role model. Thus, intensive cooperation in this area between the EU and South Africa can provide significant impetus to further this issue. Another important addition to the agreement is the joint fight against the production, trade and mass possession of so-called small arms and light weapons. Considering that around 875 million small arms and light weapons circulate around the world, we see that close cooperation is particularly important here. I also expressly welcome the agreement between the EU and South Africa in this Amendment Agreement on enhanced cooperation in the full support of the International Criminal Court and its work. I therefore very much hope that we will be able to cooperate intensively and profitably for both sides. Ladies and gentlemen, due to an editorial error in the government template concerning the procurement of the State Treaty, the agreement between the EU and its Member States and the Republic of South Africa, the following corrections should be read: ‘2. The Bulgarian, Danish, English, Estonian, Finnish, French, Greek, Italian, Latvian, Lithuanian, Maltese, Dutch, Polish, Portuguese, Romanian, Slovak, Slovenian, Spanish, Swedish, Czech and Hungarian language versions of the present State Treaty are to be notified, in accordance with Article 49(2) of the Federal Constitutional Act, by giving them public access to the Federal Ministry of European and International Affairs.’ Thank you.

It has already been said: South Africa is the EU's largest trading partner in Africa. European exports reached an annual volume of €21.5 billion by 2010. Overall, the bilateral trade volume has increased significantly since 2004, by more than a third. The Agreement contains provisions on the International Criminal Court, on disarmament, non-proliferation of weapons of mass destruction, on combating terrorism and its financing, on combating organised crime, on the prevention of mercenary activities and on trade in small arms. An in-depth political dialogue on migration is also planned. The second proposal concerns the Republic of Korea. ==References== It was signed by Ashton High Representative, the EU Member States and the Korean Foreign Minister in May 2010. It is the first such agreement between the European Union and an industrialized country. This Agreement aims to strengthen cooperation in the fields of policy, regional and international organisations, economic development, sustainable development, education and culture, law, freedom and security, as well as tourism, public administration and statistics. The third bilateral agreement on cultural cooperation between Austria and the Republic of Macedonia was also signed with the Macedonian Minister for Culture in October 2010. Foreign culture is especially important to us. Art and culture from Austria are known and appreciated all over the world, and our cultural institutions do excellent work. The objective of this Agreement is to promote cultural cooperation between Austria and Macedonia, and the cost of implementing it is quite limited, to € 5 000 per year to € 6 000. I think all three agreements are good and important, and I therefore call for approval of these three proposals. - Thank you.

This agreement is one of the first between the European Union and an industrialized country and deals with cooperation in the fields of policy, regional and international organisations, economic development, sustainability, education, culture, law, freedom, security, as well as tourism, public administration and statistics.
